ParlaMint-AT_2000-07-05-021-XXI-NRSITZ-00032_d7e4981	Mr President. Federal Chancellor! Ladies and gentlemen! The European Union Summit of Feira in June and the end of the Portuguese Presidency of the European Union were a great disappointment for Austria and its hopes for equal treatment. We therefore ask you today, Mr Bundeskanzler, and thus the whole Federal Government: How will the Austrian Federal Government deal with this? How's it going? Ladies and gentlemen! Just five months ago, a new government was praised. This government has quickly and determinedly undertaken reforms and implemented a reform policy for Austria at an incredible speed, despite the unjustified sanctions of the 14 Member States of the European Union. [[Congratulations on the ÖVP and the Freeman.]] These sanctions did not prevent the government from doing so, but they were a clot on the leg. Nevertheless, it has succeeded very quickly to decide a budget for the year 2000 and the equality of workers and employees – a decade project! – finally implement. We have carried out the privatisation and removed the state-owned companies from the state influence and the post-chacher. We have liberalized rents, we have financed a tax reform that we decided last year, so that every Austrian and every Austrian has more money in their pocket this year. We have done all of this – despite these sanctions! [[Congratulations on the ÖVP and the Freeman.]] Ladies and gentlemen! In these three days, we decide on a pension reform, a truly social pension reform that will ensure life opportunities for the older generation. [[Ironic hustle and bustle at the SPÖ]] – We know, ladies and gentlemen of the Social Democrats, that you are laughing at such questions, but it is serious for us to do so! [[Congratulations on the ÖVP and the Freeman.]] – This pension reform also allows young people to expect a pension. With this pension reform, all – and this is truly social – generations are safe. [[Abg. Ritsmer: Can you still look in the mirror?]] We decide to finance health insurance, we decide to liberalise the field of electricity and gas, and we adopt direction-setting environmental laws. We have done all of this despite the sanctions of the EU-14, and yet, ladies and gentlemen, these sanctions must be removed as soon as possible. [[Congratulations on the ÖVP and the Freeman.]] The Federal Government has decided an action programme which was very simple. 18 points were processed: Information, diplomacy, quiet diplomacy, media work, contacts with many citizens inside and outside our country. And we have succeeded in bringing two of the three institutions of the European Union to our side. The European Parliament has opposed these sanctions, and the European Commission has spoken against them. And more than half of all Europeans in all Member States of the European Union are opposed to these sanctions. This is also a result of the information work of the Federal Government. [[Congratulations on the ÖVP and the Freeman.]] But, ladies and gentlemen, the decisive step has not been taken: The 14 EU Member States have not yet decided to correct the mistakes they have made in imposing these sanctions. And that's why Feira was a disappointment. Ladies and gentlemen! Although the setting up of the Council of Wise Men is a wise step, I must give the right to say that it might have been good if the EU-14 had consulted a wise council before they imposed these sanctions on Austria, because then they would not have taken this step. [[Abg. Auer: That would have been wise!]] We are now waiting for the report of the Council of Wise Men, but there is no timetable, and since the current President of the Council of the European Union, the French Prime Minister Jospin, in turn said that the sanctions imposed against Austria would not be reduced, and also meant that there would be nothing going on over the next six months, we will continue to implement our action programme. And this provides that we are preparing today for a People's Survey, within the framework of which we ask the Austrians to share with us the great project of a reform of Europe's human rights, democracy, the rule of law and equality and to work together on this path for a just and peaceful Europe. [[Congratulations on the ÖVP and the Freeman.]] Ladies and gentlemen! The Feira Summit showed: One can want what one wants in Europe, as long as the sanctions of the EU-14 exist, to talk about nothing other than that. And it was the great disappointment in Feira that the great peace project Europe, the enlargement of the European Union, could not be discussed, because we always had to talk about the sanctions imposed on Austria. We want to spare the French Presidency of the Council of the European Union that at the next summit in Biarritz only Austria will be discussed, and that is why we are doing everything – and this also serves this public opinion – that still before the Biarritz Summit on 13 March. These sanctions will be finally abolished on October. [[Congratulations on the ÖVP and the Freeman.]] Ladies and gentlemen! This National Council, the Austrian People's Office, has the right to instruct the Federal Government to push forward the constitutional reform of the European Union. This concerns human rights, fundamental freedoms, democracy and a rule of law in the event of accusations against a country. We have the right to order the Federal Government to do so, and we have the right to lead the Federal Government here as well. Federal Chancellor! We, the Freeman and the Austrian People's Party, are today proposing that the People's Survey be conducted. And the stronger the support of this poll will be, the stronger the federal government will be to reform Europe in order to further shape Europe into a European Union in which all Member States can work and work together peacefully and do not give the tone to two or three states and are all the other less right. [[Congratulations on the ÖVP and the Freeman.]] Ladies and gentlemen! The aim of the poll is to get rid of the unjust sanctions! The aim of the People's Survey is, however, to get the support of the Austrian people for this transformation of the European Union, for this further development of the European Union. If, by the end of September, the report of the wise men in whom we have a lot of confidence and hope, we will only need this popular survey if the sanctions continue to exist. However, even if we do not need the People's Survey, we will nevertheless follow with all available means the objectives we express in this People's Survey – Europe of Law, Europe of the Rule of Law, Europe of Human Rights, Europe of Equal Rights, a peaceful Europe – including binding recommendations from the National Council to the Federal Government. That is to say, the objectives underlying this population survey are valuable in themselves and will be pursued by us. We want to take the first step today to improve Austrian position in Europe. We want to strengthen the government's fight against sanctions against Austria. Ladies and gentlemen! The discussion is open from today. It is about the project "European Constitutional Reform", it is about finally getting rid of the sanctions of the EU-14, which are unwarranted, and it is about a peaceful, united, new and large Europe. Let's all go this way together!

This opens the way for the objectivization of the procedure – away from the political will. But it is a pity and a real chance that there is neither a fixed deadline for completing the report nor much less a fixed end to sanctions against Austria. That is why this is actually a farce. I would like to say something about this report, which we did not agree with – we agreed to the mandate of Luzius Wildhaber – as well: It is not at all a problem for us to cooperate with whoever wants to be documented what this government is doing, but it is actually a European unique that the evolution and nature of a party – in this case the FPÖ – should be assessed. I expressly say at this point: I would like to thank colleagues from the Freedom Party for working together with the three wise men in this context. Of course it's not! Consider how You would respond if outsiders were to report on the nature of your party! We rely much more on the independent Austrian Constitutional Court, which, from an old age, monitors the quality of Austrian democracy and evaluates parties and evaluates whether they are admitted or not. But whoever is allowed to vote, ladies and gentlemen, has the right to be in a state or a federal government if there is a free majority on the grounds of elections. That's the beard law in Austria! [[Lively condolences with the ÖVP and the liberals.]] So we have come up with a step towards Europe, we are ready to cooperate. But now we also expect the EU-14 to cooperate with Luzius Wildhaber by complying with his wish for this report to be presented as soon as possible. I believe that the date "after the summer break" would be a very appropriate and good deadline, and I have no doubt that this report will confirm the absolute credibility of government work and the democratic legitimacy of the Austrian parties, including the FPÖ. And we expect the sanctions to be lifted as soon as possible, at least before the EU summit in Biarritz. It is now up to the EU-14 to take seriously its own proposal to get out of this deadlock and to cooperate with the President of the European Court of Human Rights, ladies and gentlemen. [[Congratulations on the ÖVP and the Freeman.]] However, because there was no end to sanctions against Austria, it is also quite clear that we want to implement our joint action plan – and it is a common action plan; I say this very clearly. Maybe you start getting used to it: If, after a long, careful examination, we make a proposal, then we are really trying to put it into force, with reason and with good arguments, and so it will be in this people's survey. We are giving the EU-14 time to the EU summit in Biarritz – that is enough time, that is four months from now. In four months, one thing you have introduced into the telephone chains in two days can be stopped without further delay with the help of the three wise men. And it is high time for an end to sanctions against Austria! [[Congratulations on the ÖVP and the Freeman.]] Today we will present you with a block of six questions, which can then be assessed by the Austrian people with "Yes" or "No". These six questions do not include a veto, in order to say it very clearly, the word "EU enlargement" is not at all present. I am saying it because today a large daily newspaper even makes a headline. These six questions are directed solely for something, for an end to sanctions against Austria, for the right of a country to elect its government in free elections, for the fundamental rights of citizens and for a clear legal position of the political parties – a long-standing demand of the European Parliament – for the equality of the EU Member States and, of course, for the rights of small states in the EU as well as Austria, and we are not just fighting for us. We want to promote a new division of tasks between Europe and the nation-states and the upgrading of the regions, as well as a legal procedure in the event of a risk of risk or violation of European obligations. There should never be a case like Austria again. That should also be the teaching of this popular survey and this change! [[Congratulations on the ÖVP and the Freeman.]] In fact, these six points, on which the Austrian people can find "Yes" or "No", are the summary of a European constitution. If President Jacques Chirac has called on the German Bundestag and the European Parliament to develop a European constitution and then to submit to the referendum of the peoples, we shall do so the other way round: We will now present these key elements of a forthcoming and existing European constitution to the people and ask for as clear a support as possible so that we can bring something into motion for Europe that has nothing to do with blocking. [[Congratulations on the ÖVP and the Freeman.]] Now the final sentence: We would all like to use our energy not for the issue of sanctions or for their abolition, but for those issues that are important to all of us – as today, for the guarantee of a first-class social system, for young people today, for a fair distribution of social transfers, for a reasonable budgetary policy that ceases to make debt, and for a reform of the Austrian administration. [[Rejoicing at the SPÖ]] – That is why we are going to do so, and despite the sanctions imposed on Austria, we will continue to address precisely these key issues. Thank you.

We have entered the discussion with a common objective of 15, namely: We all want to achieve a complete taxation of capital income in Europe! There have always been two models in this area: the one for source taxation and the one for the exchange of information. At the beginning, Austria, together with Belgium, Greece and Luxembourg, represented the position of source taxation and thus co-existence, because we know that the functioning Austrian model of capital income tax, which has proved successful for Austria and for the population, could become a European model. We have represented this model of source taxation because we knew that it is compatible with the banking secrecy for the Austrians, who are all the more important to our country after the change in anonymity. We have represented this model because we want Austria to remain an attractive financial place, a capital place and that does not leave Austria or Europe. [[Congratulations with the liberals and representatives of the ÖVP.]] We landed on Sunday at 4 p.m., entered the negotiations at 5 p.m. and negotiated until Tuesday, 1 p.m., with all the consequence Austrian positions, Austrian interests represented in Feira. Ladies and gentlemen! We have achieved a success in the matter! In contrast to Luxembourg, Belgium and Greece, we did not give up our position, but we used something there, which should be of course: We said: Austria is an EU member country with all obligations and all rights!, and we have strongly represented the Austrian position there, we have not surrendered! [[Congratulations with the liberals and the ÖVP.]] We said: We must keep the banking secret, we must ensure the taxation of sources for Austria. Ladies and gentlemen - we have achieved this for the first time in a European document - have also succeeded in ensuring that the banking secret for the population of Austria is established. We have achieved that the source tax is fixed for Austrians. We have achieved that the banking secret for foreigners is also accepted by Europe as a constitutional one. We have thus achieved a success in the matter for our country through sustainable, consistent representation of our rights. ParlaMint-AT_2000-07-05-021-XXI-NRSITZ-00032_d7e12584	Mr President. High house! Mrs Petrovic, in the Constitutional Committee and also today in the Government's plenary, has made the allegations that we want to make the constitutional judges inclined by making their own survivors' arrangement for them. There is a dubious legal situation on this point. I can inform you of the opinion of the Head of the Constitutional Service on this problem. It says – I quote – that: "If one considers the regulation of § 5i Constitutional Court Law to be a final one, a different reduction regime would not be permitted at a simple legal level both above and below a ministerial reference." – And it goes on to say: "However, the text of § 5i Abs. 1 Constitutional Court Act does not preclude simply providing for a reduction regime below the maximum amount provided for in § 5i Constitutional Court Law." - Quote. We will now choose the second solution. But this is a dubious question, which is confirmed by the head of the Constitutional Service itself. Mrs. Dr. Petrovic has always supported this in the Constitutional Committee, and she has asked: What does the head of the Constitutional Service say about this? – So even this one says that there can be two different views here. However, it was in no way the Federal Government's intention to prefer the judges of the Constitutional Court – or their survivors – and to make them more inclined to the new pension scheme. Mrs Reitsamer! They mentioned the financial impact of the increase in the pension contribution of 0.8 percent. They have pointed out the financial impact on pensioners and have spoken in this context of a billion. [[Abg. Reiter: No, I have spoken about the financial consequences of the pension adjustment, Mr State Secretary!]] - That's right, just from the pension adjustment. I just wanted to say that in the public service, the entire pension adjustment, which we hope will be decided today in line with the future guarantee of the pension system, will bring a reduction in the planned increase of around 4 billion schilling in 2003. And the contribution of the Federal Pensionists with the moderate increase of 0.8 percent will make up about 500 million schilling. I believe that this is a major contribution to this pension system, because it is also necessary to take into account the relative amount of pension schemes of the Federal Pensioners. As we know, the re-regulation of pension systems is being considered throughout Europe. At each Euro-meeting, at each OECD Council of Ministers, the call on the Member States to ensure that pension systems are safeguarded in a sustainable manner. Why are they sustainable? – We have higher life expectancy, but a declining birth rate. They enter the profession later on due to longer periods of training. Moreover, due to the increased prosperity, a trend towards basic care is apparent, and on a voluntary basis, self-sufficiency is to be made for higher pension prospects. One can now be of different opinion on the financialability of a pension system, but it will always be on the following three points – there is no fourth, fifth, or sixth points – to be nailed: How high is the pension? How long do I take the pension? Which contributions do I make? A comparison of the Austrian system with those reforms made abroad shows that, although we did not implement the last of several pension reforms until 1997, as far as the retirement age is concerned, nothing has happened from the legal obligation, but only incentives for a voluntary increase have been created – and this, although experts have already indicated since 1993 that life expectancy is currently every diennium, thank God for increasing by two years and therefore the retirement age should be increased by analogous to that! At the time, only a very gentle and very long-term scheme was implemented in the public service – for example, 80 percent of the final pension, if the full calculation basis was reached – which, starting from 2003, slowly increases the calculation period until 2019 in accordance with the current calculation period. We have therefore not intervened very effectively in this respect. And with regard to the government's share of the contributions, I have to say that that "third" has been permanently dismantled before the start of this government. Moreover, there is no natural law or there is no law under which contributions have to be financed by a certain state share, and, of course, one was very generous in the recognition of non-contributory retirement periods. I would now like to address improvements to the public service in relation to the government template. We have had very long and intensive discussions with all four public trade unions – if you add the artists, that is, the members of the Bundestheater, who have their own trade union after the disfellowshipping, there are five public trade unions. The first, namely the railways, have sounded out from the outset, saying: Our pension reform was bought in 1997 with a higher contribution, we do not go along with it, we are not at all ready to talk at all. At a later time, when they saw that this was not good in the public, they were ready to talk, but a strike of the duration of an hour had already been announced. On this famous day of action, the trains are stopped for an hour – under this pressure we should negotiate again. However, they were not willing to take up the retirement age, which, under the existing 35-year scheme and the fact that a large number of railway workers have just entered at the age of 18, is 53 years, voluntarily to increase for a year and a half. In the case of such a voluntary increase, we had offered to talk about the further reduction of pension contributions with regard to the scheme of the others at 61.5 years and to create a bonus system. Although the trade union was ready for a bonus system, it was not prepared to accept a legal regulation or a mandatory regulation on the increase of 35 years of service required for retirement to 36.5 years of service. That's the stand. The postal services have almost the next signed out, because they are currently having difficulties in their businesses at all. What has happened in these companies so far? – In the post office, for many years, there was an excessive level of personnel, there was no preparation for the free market at all, Deutsche Telekom now has the biggest difficulties in the stock exchange due to the high level of staff, one has not prepared for competition, one has only accumulated debts – these debts were needed to ensure that the surpluses from the phone fees were transferred to the budget. So they were very soon no longer ready to continue negotiations. Finally, only two trade unions remain, namely the Public Service Trade Union and the Municipal Workers' Trade Union. It was then possible to see some occasion enough for the representative of the parish staff – that is, because of the famous "side-letter" – to appear as well. But what is the fearsome thing in this "side-letter"? – It was just what has already been enshrined in the government programme, namely that the government is considering standardising the pension system and that it wants to introduce a three-pillar model, i.e. basic care, occupational care, self-sufficiency. We from the government were even willing to suspend the planned increase in the pension contribution of 0.8 percent to start discussions as soon as possible. To this end, it must be said that trade union representatives have constantly called for such talks, because they have always said: Now comes a first pension reform, what does the next one look like? We want to know everything for the next legislative period! And after we have said that, the representative of the parish staff has also signed out. Finally, the representative of the Public Service Union has remained the only one. The pressure of the other trade unions on him has been noted: Just don't agree, because you're in violation of solidarity! – We had already agreed on content in many areas, although they always kept us there, we didn’t like the social partners, and there were no talks either. There's been intense conversations! And although it has not been concluded, the following rules are included in an amendment that is still being tabled today – they include some of the agreements already reached in the negotiations –: One-sided retirement will only be possible on the basis of important service interests, which is the same as in the case of a transfer and thus legally secured. Thus, the employee can continue to retire from 61.5 years of age with a simple declaration, which requires no important interest in the service. This provision is not granted to the public service provider, which must have an important interest in the service. We have offered this in the sense of harmonisation, as a contribution to peace, although in the end we have not received any agreement. In addition, there will be further exceptions to the reduction scheme, which is particularly important for the executive. In the event of incapacity for service after an accident in the performance of the service, there will also be an exception to the deduction arrangements. These are known to be increased from two to three percent per year before the retirement age of 61.5 years. In the event of a service accident, there will also be a derogation. Finally, there will also be an improved transitional system for postal staff with early retirement models. Those already included in the early retirement model will de facto be able to continue to use it. In order to ensure that this is as gentle as possible for businesses and thus at least Deutsche Telekom can or will be encouraged to use its stock exchange, there will also be a system which will satisfy all interests. We have proved that we can talk to the social partners and that there is something going on during discussions. - Thank you.

ParlaMint-AT_2000-07-05-021-XXI-NRSITZ-00032_d7e17342	Mr President. High house! This government stands for social security. [[Abg. [[Congratulations with the liberals and the ÖVP.]] [[Abg. You're right, blue is the color of warmth!]] It is precisely because we want to maintain this system that it is high time for reforms to know You're as good as we are. Therefore, with the Social Rights Amendment Act, we have put in place a package for the preservation of this system in three ways: Ensuring the pension system, reform of social security in a first step and rehabilitation of health insurance. First of all, the pension system: I would like to emphasize very clearly what my previous speakers have done, that we are in favour of maintaining the system of payments and that we are in favour of maintaining the high net replacement rate. Our system was created at the beginning of the fifties, at a time when there were quite different conditions. People now start working later, but go to retirement earlier, i.e. they work shorter. Pensions are higher, and fortunately people live longer. We have the lowest retirement age compared to the other EU countries, and at the same time we have a system that allows for very high pensions. Therefore: If we want to secure pensions for the current pensioners and in the future, and thus ensure prosperity in the future, then a reform is urgent. It was necessary to increase the early retirement age by 1.5 years and to raise the reductions, which are only increased by 1 percentage point. This has created a socially acceptable reform. It will finally implement what the governments did not do before. We are thus fulfilling the opinions of international experts, all of whom say the same thing indisputably, and this will also achieve a long-term alignment with the EU. As has often been stressed and as you know very well, colleagues from the opposition, this reform is very moderate and socially compatible. We have a gentle grinding control. We have introduced a curing clause for cases which may be more affected. We have introduced a safeguard clause. There is no intervention in existing pensions. All occupational groups are equally affected and men and women are also equally affected, which can be clearly demonstrated. We have created an improved professional protection for all occupational groups, whether they are trained or unskilled workers, whether they are traders or farmers. And for particularly sensitive professional groups, such as construction workers, nothing changes at all. This reform implements the objective set by the pension reforms of the 1990s, namely an increase in the actual retirement age. All the reforms so far have not achieved this objective, on the contrary, it has been shown that people in Austria are still retired earlier. In 1993, they retired with an average of 58.4 years and in 1998 they were 57.4 years old. That is, the retirement age fell by a whole year in this short period of time. I regret that the Chamber of Labour and the Federation of Trade Unions did not really make a constructive contribution, because the package, which is constantly being received, with its employment and health policies, is just a flanking measure and cannot be put in place of a pension reform. I can say that we have developed a common platform at the last meeting of the social partners in a very friendly consensus, in which the proposals of the employee representatives are also to be dealt with. A first meeting has already taken place. In this sense, I would like to appeal to you, ladies and gentlemen of the opposition, to finally accept reason and to agree to this pension reform, to take over this moderate and very well-coordinated package! This is a system we want to maintain! There are studies that clearly state what should have happened long ago in order to put this system on an economically better basis. This has not happened so far, so this reform has become necessary today. Social insurance companies manage billions of contributions from insured persons, and it is therefore necessary that we introduce instruments which each private company has long seen and introduced as self-evident. It is about strengthening the efficiency test, and it is about early detection of misdevelopments. Therefore, this bill provides for a controlling facility which enables the main association to develop towards a holding company, which has been urgently needed for a long time. This will make it possible to make a better use of the individual carriers, which will enable greater networking of the carriers and better cooperation between them. As a result, savings will be made, which will lead to synergies between the effects of use and will increase efficiency. This will use and manage the contributions of insured persons more economically and sparingly. My concern is to support social security in this modernisation, so we provide it with a modern, business-oriented tool with which it can better pursue these objectives. Of the planned measures, we can say that they are aimed at strengthening the cost awareness of the beneficiaries and, of course, at controlling them, towards the cheaper settled area, away from the more expensive ambulatories. It will, of course, be necessary to strengthen the established area as a accompanying measure. [[Congratulations on the ÖVP and the Freeman.]] In this letter, I drew the attention of Prime Minister Zeman to the fact that no measures are to be taken that are irreversible until all issues relating to the environmental impact assessment and the safety of this power plant are clarified convincingly and legally. I have called for the Czech Republic to ratify and apply the Espoo Convention this year, as provided for in the Accession Partnership to the Union, and I have recalled that the European Union has requested regular and comprehensive information on the construction programme and the granting of the works permit from the Czech Republic. – To date, however, such information has not been available to the Union. Foreign Minister Ferrero-Waldner, after Minister for the Environment Molterer, also took the initiative in this regard, and she pointed out, both in a discussion with the Commissioner responsible for enlargement Verheugen and with the Czech Foreign Minister Kavan, that the question of the competitive admissibility of promoting temelin, the completion of the TKW Temelin, is quite a topic for the European Union's competition policy. Foreign Minister Kavan's answer was: There is currently a decision on the completion, but not yet for the start-up of the Temelin nuclear power station. However, we are vigilant, for we know exactly that in truth – you are right – it can only be a matter of days, until the step takes place from completion in the structural sense until commissioning. Now to the individual questions addressed to me. Question No 1 by ElWOG. The new novel, the recast of § 13 ElWOG, is a significant improvement in the Austrian anti-atomic policy, because, contrary to the previous legal situation, a single supply contract is no longer applied, but, in line with the physical situation, the assessment of the admissibility of electricity imports to the overall mix of the supply in the third country concerned is carried out. Therefore, for the purpose of our domestic demand coverage, electricity imports should be prohibited in general if, first, these third countries produce electrical energy to meet their needs also in installations that do not comply with the state of the art – an important point! – or which poses a direct or indirect risk to life or health, to humans, animals and plants, and if, secondly, these countries do not provide proof of the proper disposal of the waste arising from the production of electrical energy, or if they cannot provide a concept for the waste now or later. A request from NGOs was therefore met, and I very much hope that on the basis of the recasting of the ElWOG electricity imports for domestic demand coverage from the Czech Republic from 1st century onwards. No more will take place in October 2001. Questions 2a to 2c, to the anti-dumping rules. Of course, the anti-dumping rules are not very different within the EU Member States, because they are Community law. It is also difficult to imagine how a purely national anti-dumping policy can work, and this is particularly true of electricity, in a single market where products are freely circulated after their first in-vehicle bridge. Therefore, Austria has also taken action within the EU for effective anti-dumping rules as well as for an appropriate decision in the individual case. And we also want to stick to this policy, but we should not hide the fact that the individual Member States have, in part, serious deviations in their individual positions, ranging from protectionist-determined viewpoints to a completely unlimited liberalisation. This is why our very careful and balanced pragmatic access to an anti-dumping policy is of particular importance. Question 3: The amendment to the ElWOG provides for a complete liberalisation of the electricity market and includes the supply of electrical energy in all variants. There is freedom of choice for consumers, and this means that they can choose their suppliers freely. However, in addition to the information on the price, the selection criteria of the dealers include those relating to the sources of which the electricity dealer, supplier or producer carries its energy. Each supplier can apply for its products with different specifications, whereby in future a very essential product feature will be the origin of electrical energy. In order to make this credible, a record obligation is provided for so-called labelling, which is subject to a permanent regulatory review. This labelling is based on the understanding of the transactions carried out in the field of electricity trading; this method is used to identify the sources of the economic advantage on the basis of the quantities supplied and, ultimately, by percentage. The pressure of the powerful consumer will, of course, result in a pressure on dealers in the medium term to show as small a share as possible under the heading "nuclear production". On the part of NGOs – this is very important, and I would like to thank very much for it – and on the part of all the anti-atomic organisations active in Austria, which have been intensively involved in this discussion on labelling and have been consulted, this regulation, which has been found and is now available to you, is expressly welcomed. This can also be clearly read in many press releases. This will make hydropower energy a very favourable form of production for electricity in the long term. – I cannot see the problem that you have in mind on this question that there is a financial burden on those consumers who do not want to obtain nuclear power. In addition, in a fully liberalised energy market, it would be a very difficult task to re-evaluate additional burdens, which are hardly recoverable – the consumer has freedom of choice. Questions 4a and 4b: Energy economic cooperation with the reform states of Central Europe is one of the priorities of this Federal Government's nuclear policy. However, bilateral cooperation also requires the will of both partners. This has sometimes been lacking in the past. It has now succeeded in creating a viable basis for an energy partnership with the Czech Republic. Some projects were completed at the end of April, such as a cooperation exchange and a conference on renewable energy sources, which took place with financial support from the Federal Government and the state of Lower Austria in St. Pölten. In the same way, at European level, we are committed to the priority of macroeconomic reforms at all levels with a view to making the energy sector more sustainable and taking particular account of the potential of renewable energy sources, and of course we will continue to do so in the future. [[Congratulations on the ÖVP and the Freeman.]] Questions 5a and 5b relating to this fictitious authorisation procedure for Temelin together with Germany: After intensive informal contacts – and thus the accusation of a multi-month delay – with regard to a fictitious authorisation procedure for Temelin under German nuclear law had already been made correspondingly in autumn 1999. The German Government officially informed us in December 1999 that it does not consider a fictitious authorisation procedure to be an appropriate means of promoting the efforts of the candidate countries to increase nuclear safety to the level of the Union. – Perhaps a conversation with the Environment Minister Trittin is helpful, because that was his answer. I would add that, in this context, we are focusing our efforts since the end of last year on once again bilaterally contacting the Czech Republic in order to obtain the information that will enable us to assess whether the state of the art in the European Union is actually being achieved. I recall that the common position of the Union on Chapter 14, Energy, which calls on the Czech Republic – I quote – to provide "regularly comprehensive information on the construction plan and the procedures for granting the operating permit for the Temelin vehicle based on the final interpretation of the plant". To our regret, the Czech Republic has so far failed to comply with this call, despite the opposite announcement. This will be an issue during my visit to the European Commission on 12 February. July. Question No 5c: The Federal Government will use all available means – and will do so in the future – to convince the Czech Republic that the start-up of the Temelin nuclear power plant is a massive failure with long-term negative effects. If this truck is to operate, compliance with the state of the art in the Union is essential. In other words: Temelin must, in the unfortunate case of its commissioning; theoretically at least, be authorised as a new plant in the Union according to today's safety criteria. Here, according to the government programme, the agreed position of the Federal Government is clear and precise – I quote –: "In any event, without prejudice to the objective of Austria to achieve the elimination of nuclear power plants, the maximum safety standards shall be applied in relation to those at the border or planned level." On question 6: I have already responded to the assumption that nothing has been implemented. With the Anti-Atom Policy Action Plan, the Federal Government has set itself very ambitious targets at the time. You know that, and we know that. With pressure and compulsion, we cannot achieve anything, but only with conviction and also with incentives. The new Federal Government, in its government programme, reaffirmed the determination to continue its implementation of this action plan. The fact that there are now closure plans for all non-retrofitable nuclear power plants in the acceding countries – although the actual closing data are not satisfactory – is not least due to the very consistent attitude of Austria. As regards the Espoo Convention, it should be pointed out that the current accession partnership with the Czech Republic points out the "complete implementation and effective application of the Environmental Impact Assessment Directive" as a short-term priority, thus requiring the corresponding steps of the Czech Republic still in 2000. This too is an Austrian initiative. Question 7: The consistent attitude of the Federal Government has contributed significantly to the difficult completion process for the Temelin car. It should be remembered: Already last year, on the basis of significant objections from Austrian experts, the forthcoming decision on completion was initially postponed and, as you yourself have said, made by very narrow majority. Without prejudice to the truly worrying development of the past few days, it must be noted that a number of procedures are still pending, in particular the ongoing EIA procedure. The Federal Government has created the best possible conditions for the participation of Austrian citizens, as well as – and I can see this without exaggeration – the most comprehensive and well-founded expert opinion in this process. Questions 8 and 9: I have today sent a letter to the Czech Prime Minister Zeman, in which I urgently asked him not to take irreversible steps. I also called for a comprehensive EIA for the entire facility and the immediate ratification and application of the Espoo Convention by the Czech Republic. I asked Zeman to contact the Minister for the Environment of Austria and the Czech Republic immediately in order to discuss the further procedure. Question 10: Ms Ferrero-Waldner, Minister of State, has worked intensively within the framework of her responsibilities. In concrete terms, the Austrian ambassador was instructed last month to hand over an aid mémoire to the Austrian Federal Government. It was also held in June. The official response from the Czech Republic is still pending. The Austrian Embassy in Prague is seeking a quick response. However, Federal Minister Molterer immediately addressed his Czech staff in writing and asked for urgent information. I very much hope that this will help to achieve a solution. In any case, however, these interventions will continue to a large extent over the next few days and weeks.

It entered into force on 1 October 2001. I therefore believe that we do not have to blame Parliament for this. The preparatory work in this direction is good and will work in a sustainable way. ParlaMint-AT_2000-07-05-021-XXI-NRSITZ-00032_d7e19417	Mr President. Federal Chancellor! Federal Minister! High house! Dear ladies and gentlemen! Temelin has been a hot topic for me, my home community and my home region since the beginning of construction, because it is in good weather in the sight of my home community. The construction of this nuclear power plant in my home region has led to the hottest discussions long before the fall of the Iron Curtain. However, it has also led to an intensive discussion of the issue of energy and energy policy and has also set quite tangible examples in practice. We realized comprehensive energy concepts and not just put them on the table in theory. This means that all energy saving potentials have been used as far as possible and the remaining energy needs have now been covered as far as possible by renewable energy. The special pleasure is that this model has not only been copied and acquired in my home region, but has also come to us from the Czech Republic's interested citizens – thousands of them – who have informed themselves about the alternatives to those technologies that have been seen during the communist period as the only solution to the brown coal problem of our Czech neighbor. It is particularly gratifying that a partner community of our home region has now realized such projects and is becoming a multiplier in the Czech Republic. For me, the generation of nuclear energy is personally a technology, a form of energy production, which I consider to be, unfortunately, still legal form of crime. It is simply not possible for me to imagine that nuclear energy is dominant and responsible. It is therefore categorically opposed. At the latest from the time of the catastrophic accident in Chernobyl, we were told what enormous suffering is caused for life and body beyond generations. The exorbitant economic damage has not yet been taken into account. Nuclear energy is simply and simply a robbery of the opportunities of future generations over centuries and therefore, from my point of view, no further topic of discussion. If the Czech government is now trying to put the Temelin nuclear power station into operation with all its force, then the Czech Republic must be aware that it will be able to breathe into a technical dinosaur life – the most dangerous life – and thus encounter strict rejection in Austria. The world is now considering the exit from nuclear energy, and it is also being acted in this direction. In our neighbouring country, the Federal Republic of Germany, such an exit has recently been decided. [[Abg. Schwarzenberger: In 32 years!]] Unfortunately, after the collapse of the Greens in the Federal Republic, it will take a period of time, which some of us may regret will no longer experience. With regard to the negative attitude towards nuclear energy, Austria has the highest degree of credibility in the world that a country can offer at all – Kreisky thank you! In the course of a vote, the Austrian population categorically rejected nuclear energy and made Zwentendorf a long-term economic nuclear power plant, but also made it the most expensive reminder against nuclear energy. The only chain reaction that gives reason is a chain reaction of the movement of the population, the citizens by simply realizing in a process of opinion that this is the wrong way. That's what we all have to work on. Austria's energy policy is based on sustainability and fair sustainability. This includes the serious use of our hydropower to a reasonable extent. And a significant part of Austrian energy is now produced from biomass. We are top-ranking in this area in Europe. These opportunities can be used much more intensively in the future. But it will be up to all of us to create the appropriate framework conditions to implement this in practice. We have a highest degree of credibility on the one hand. We have parade examples with which we demonstrate that it is already happening in this form. It is only about doing this together and implementing it. What I regret is allegations of a complicity with which Austrian companies and Austrians are accused, if – it is generally known – a French group of states has serious interest in Temelin, thereby proving who is in Europe for these false technologies. The task of reorientating energy policy is not only one for Austria – here we are pioneers – but this task must be primarily a topic in the European area. France is a disastrous final light in this regard, because it has the highest share of nuclear power in Europe, if not worldwide. It is important here to provide Europe-wide guidelines for what is necessary in the context of technical standards and also to clarify what liabilities become active when incidents occur. This alone would bring some investors in the right direction in the future, because at present there are no insurance companies or the like for such power plants. I would also like to make it clear here. I think it is surprising, and it is easy to reject the fact that we are voting against our Chancellor in a way that he would be the Czech Chancellor and that he would have the responsibility from his point of view. If it is also claimed here that the subject is not a chief issue, then I am very surprised that Mr. Van der Bellen is hiding behind his folders today and is not here at the speaker's desk, if it is the chief thing of the Greens. I would like to say that it makes sense to recognize the situation and find a common way forward, not only for Austria but also for Europe and for future generations. - Thank you.

ParlaMint-AT_2000-07-05-021-XXI-NRSITZ-00032_d7e21663	Mr President. Mrs. Vice chancellor! Mrs. Federal Minister! Dear Ladies and gentlemen of the Federal Government! Federal Minister! The new Federal Government has become known in its government programme for a sustainable rehabilitation of statutory pension insurance. A key reason for this measure was to maintain the confidence of the younger generation in statutory pension schemes. The fact that there are financing problems in the Austrian pension system has been known for more than a decade. All the studies and opinions prepared between 1991 and 2000 clearly show a major problem, namely, a much too low age of retirement in Austria in international comparison, with increasing life expectancy and a dramatic drop in birth. As we all know, the pension reforms that have been started so far do not have a definitive solution to these problems. It is therefore essential – and in this all the experts, including across party borders, agree – to quickly take further measures to ensure the protection of old age. In the medium term, it will be necessary to unite pension systems and to change them to a three-pillar model already internationally tested, namely a model with a basic government provision, a tax-funded operating provision and an indispensable independent provision. [[Complaints with the liberals]] We all know that the long-term guarantee of the pension system cannot be done in one step or with a single law, such as that to be decided today. Further measures will undoubtedly have to follow in the coming years. As the total expenditure of pension insurance increases from year to year, although the difference between the number of persons working and the number of pensioners has not really been opened up, rapid action is inevitable. Any expectation would have meant that the necessary rehabilitation measures would have been all the more severe and tougher later. Remember that, according to the opinion of the Pension and Pension Adjustment Advisory Council, the state subsidies would be exploding to a pension insurance scheme, the Federal Government would not counter-tax from 63 billion schilling in 1999 to 70 billion schilling in 2001, 83 billion schilling in 2003 and finally to inconceivable 90 billion schilling in 2004. This was clearly at the expense of the workers and the skilled and would certainly no longer be eligible for funding from the state. I would also like to make a brief comment on two arguments which are repeatedly put forward, namely on the argument of life planning and the so-called violation of trust protection by sudden intervention into so-called well-acquired rights. Ladies and gentlemen! Over the past 12 years, the Austrians have not been able to focus their life planning on the maintenance of existing rights in the pension system, but the legislator has already intervened in this area through three reforms already during this period. If you are already straining the knowledge of the Constitutional Court, number G 172/99-8, it must also be pointed out that the Constitutional Court of the Federal Government naturally exempts you from making the legal situation different for the future. ParlaMint-AT_2000-07-05-021-XXI-NRSITZ-00032_d7e22710	Mr President. High house! Ladies and gentlemen! Ladies and gentlemen on the Government Bank! Allow me to make a few clarifications on some comments, especially from the opposition, which, as I believe, lead to misunderstandings or to difficulties in understanding. I start with the so-called recipe fee, with the increase from 45 to 55 p. They all know that the rise in drug costs is not solely due to an increased prescription practice, but also to the fact that modern, new, but more expensive drugs are used. De facto, however, is not really an increase. If you consider that in the case of gastritis, for example, it used to take about two to three weeks and that multiple prescriptions were necessary to cure this disease, you have to admit that there are drugs that are more expensive today, but are only prescribed once and lead to healing or restoring the patient's health in a week. – The latter is the first interest, the former is of additional advantage, but de facto the patient has to spend less for his illness than before. [[President Dipl.-Ing. Prinzhorn shall take over the chairmanship.]] I agree with Mr Groenewald that in the lower part of the income pyramid, or where the lower incomes are, we are getting sick sooner and more often and also living relatively shorter. Our measures have led to that very third being taken into account and excluded from the treatment fee of the ambulances. It is not the government's intention to introduce burdens here, but we want to push back the burdens in the ambulances and free them for those patients who are dependent on an ambulance visit. I may bring you examples from my own experience: Child's ambulance. – It is very important that children are also treated with pure sniffing. But if this causes doctors to be stopped or overloaded for two to three hours by the treatment of simpler things and seriously ill, cancer-sick children have to wait these two to three hours for the administration of pain-killing drugs, this is certainly the wrong way. [[Congratulations with the liberals and the ÖVP.]] I don't want to go into the cemetery comparison because I think it's cynical. De facto, however, it is the case that the 150 s should not lead to the fact that one takes a shine from the established doctor and goes into the ambulance, but we want to open a kind of gatekeeper function that has not yet taken place, namely that the patient is treated as far as possible with the established doctor and does not even have to visit the ambulance. I can explain this to you by means of a short example; perhaps you will understand what it really is all about. There is an ambulance study carried out in 1995 by a Vienna hospital, which found in a retrospective study that 75 percent of the benefits it provided during a given period could also have been provided in the same way to the established physician. In 1996, in that year, as well as in the last time the health insurance companies were asked to cash out through strong increases in contributions from the then main government party, the municipality of Vienna was 3.5 billion schilling in the Wiener Hospitals. If one assumes that about 75 percent of the benefits can actually be provided to the established doctor, this means an extra effort of 2.7 billion schilling. At the same time, however, only 560 million Schillings have been paid in by the social security institution as a whole, so that only 17 percent were actually replaced by the health insurance provider, while 3 billion Schillings were practically self-retaining the taxpayer. If you convert this now, you will see that, due to the tariff policy and even if the social security provider was fully paid – which can be estimated with 1 billion schilling – here a net savings of 1.8 billion schilling have resulted in more than 50 percent of the total cost being saved – from tax money, and that is again the self-retaining of all citizens. At the same time, so to speak, that the madness still has a point, 960 million Schillings were spent this year by the municipality of Vienna to expand their ambulances in order to pay for the increased inflow. From then until today, we have again recorded an increase of 22 percent. This is a major cost, and it is important to take action here. At the same time, this year a private investment of 350 million Schilling was made by the locals, which did not cost any large amounts of tax money, because this amount was included in the tariffs and was made by these private individuals. [[Abg. Silhavy: But who pays the rates?]] - I'll talk to you soon. However, we have also taken measures to that effect – if you read the ASVG novels carefully, you will find that wherever the established area is not yet able to perform this gatekeeper function, the ambulance can very well be visited and must not be paid for. The "Emergency" is also curated. It is set out in the KAG in length: What an emergency is still the doctor! Even here there is no difficulty to see. Finally, in the case of self-indebted handle and in the event of drunkenness, the regress has already been regulated accordingly in the stationary area and therefore no innovation in this type. Now to the so-called private ambulances. Of course, real private ambulances do not require any treatment because they do not have a contract with an insurance institution. Where there's no contract, I can't cancel it either! But if contracts exist, you know that very well, because of the legal situation, and not at the request of the operators, they are ordination-like institutions – these are physical institutes, laboratories, CT and magnetic resonance institutions – which have the advantage of working much more cost-effectively than other areas, including the infirmary facilities. I can tell you that a computer tomography there costs 1 600 s, while it costs 3 500 to 8 000 S in the public sector or at health insurance facilities, while at a magnetic resonance tomography the rates are 2 600 S in the settled area, while at the other facilities they amount to 5 000 to 13 000 S – in words: thirteen thousand schilling. To use a gatekeeper function here is, I think, an obligation. It is therefore the intention of this Federal Government not to save somewhere in the wrong place, but simply to use the resources better. The last three decades – in order to say it freely after barbecue parss – have apparently passed according to the principle: half-way with half-power to half the goal! This government, which is a new one, will go with full force all the way to the goal pursued. - Thank you many times.

It is positive that the public is very close to this question: Raised by one and a half years – is that justified, yes or no? The Commission has not yet received any comments from the Member States. We received for this proposal, I believe, not a unanimous, but a predominantly yes from the Austrian population, especially from the young people, but also from the older people, who know how important it is that pensions remain secure in the future and can continue to be paid out, ladies and gentlemen. That is why I regret that, even in these days, false reports have been made about the effects of the pension measures and have been spread from the speaker's desk on several occasions. Silhavy: But with shots!]] There's nothing going on here at all in the age of arrival. [[Congratulations on the ÖVP.]] [[Abg. [[Congratulations on the ÖVP.]] They are again baffling what has been introduced under Minister Hesoun, namely that there are discounts. No matter whether Professor Marin, Professor Rürup – and I could name other names – has been said over and over again, we need higher deposits. That is why the reductions will be higher in the future, but the retirement age, ladies and gentlemen, does not change at all for these people. Not a month! The third case, Mrs Wimmer's, is totally wrong, because you have not thought, Mr Verzellnich, that this Mrs Wimmer is now entitled to emergency aid and can therefore acquire additional insurance periods. This is a socially just reform, which, of course, is linked to hardships for some people, but it is socially just, it has been disbanded socially, and it also gives those who have prepared their life-planning properly, the security to be able to observe this life-planning without any significant redefinition. As we all know, these soldiers carry out security police tasks and often have to arrest illegal border workers, carry out luggage searches at strangers, and much more. If these soldiers suffer an accident in carrying out this activity in the context of border operations or if they are arrested, they will now be treated as the guardians under social law on the basis of this novelle, according to the Guards' Aid Act. This is a social preventive measure which we are very likely to owe to our young ministers. In connection with the public debate on officials, I would like to quote a voice which clearly shows the field of tension in which the public servant stands today: The constitutional lawyer Professor Heinz Mayer brings it to the point when he says – I quote it literally –: "Deserve them" – meaning the officials – "what they have? – A simple answer is not possible. From my point of view, the path to be taken is clear: Getting out of the defensive, promoting performance, and saying goodbye to those who understand the civil service career as waiting room for early retirement. This will take a long time and only succeed when politics and bureaucracy are drawing on a line. But one should fail to present the officials as blackmailers. This is wrong and unfair. However, officials and their officials must also note that they must adapt to new needs, otherwise they become a foreign body in society." - Quote. In order to prevent civil servants and civil servants from becoming a foreign body in society, we need a modern civil service law which puts the principle of service and the concept of service more in the foreground than before. With this innovation, I believe we are making an important contribution. Thank you.

ParlaMint-AT_2000-07-05-021-XXI-NRSITZ-00032_d7e25858	Mr President. Federal Minister! Ladies and gentlemen! High house! The law on the gas economy and the revision of the Electricity Economic and Organisation Act are not self-evident. The directive would have given us time to open the market to gas and electricity in a slightly longer stage than we will do with these proposals. However, the main objective of these two projects was and is to reduce electricity and gas prices for millions of households in Austria, and to reduce prices for industry and small and medium-sized enterprises – for large industrial enterprises that are already authorised customers – to a certain extent in order to secure the location. In addition to the decision-making of the ElWOG, the SP/VP government has already discussed this issue in the previous year and, in part, has also negotiated it. It was therefore only logical, I believe, that, above all, because the government parties, when they valued a high-quality implementation of the market opening, needed the consent of the required two-thirds majority to involve us in the negotiations. We have therefore expressed our views in the public as well as in the negotiations. We believe that the market opening targets are really very ambitious. In the case of gas, the market for end-users of 25 million cubic meters is already expected to be at 1. ==References== It will be opened in full October 2002. We would have preferred to see 20 million cubic metres in the first stage, because the inequalities would have been a little lower, but could be lived by this procedure. In the electricity we assumed almost the same idea. We felt that the market for small and medium-sized enterprises and for all distribution companies was already starting from 1. It could be opened in 2001 and the Minister should be empowered to open in general by regulation. But in our opinion the present solution is also quite acceptable, especially because, if all this begins to work in time, the Minister has the possibility of having already three months before the law with 1. ==References====External links== July 2001 to open completely. For all EVUs, for all in the e-business as well as in the gas industry, this process is a major challenge. We were of the opinion that this can only work if a specific regulation is installed. It is at the top of the so-called regulator. This is still held in the constitutional order to be adopted, so that there will be changes in this context only by qualified majority. Our ideas of the regulator were and are: He has to be independent. It must work quickly and efficiently in order to be able to carry out the many tasks accordingly. We were of the opinion – and I know one of the co-negotiators here – that we could get across Austria with a rule zone. However, I think we must take political note that Vorarlberg and Tyrol will remain their own regulatory zone. The rest of Austria is then summarized in another, the third, rule zone. Federal Minister Bartenstein! I have a concern in this regard: Indeed, as we were invited by various sides to do so, we have also considered whether there should be any means of avoiding the task of clearing and setting up. I now know from conversations that you assured the negotiator that this performance will be advertised by Elektroms-Control GmbH. If that is the case, then I believe we can distance ourselves from opening the negotiated package once again to include this derogation scheme, because only on the basis of the call for tender it is ensured that the best bidder will really come about. Another key point in the proposals is the field of renewable energy sources. You know, we've set an ambitious goal: By 2010 the CO2 loads of the air will be reduced by 13 percent. It is therefore necessary to further promote and support the renewable energy sector. We agreed that the area of classical renewable energy sources should be promoted at country level through feed-in schemes. In this context, the higher goal is to achieve a 4 percent share by 2007, with which mainly the requirements of biomass operators are to be met, and this is also due to the fact that we have expanded the circle of eco-power plants to be recognized in this context. Mixing fires and waste burning with a high bioenergy content were taken in, but for the first time clearly and clearly the small hydropower, based on 10 megawatts and with an objective of 8 percent, and it is also noted that the target size can be evaluated in case of need. The basis of this scheme is to provide certificates. We have also found a satisfactory solution for combined heat and power, which also makes a significant contribution to ensuring the supply of district heating in agglomeration centres, and with 31. ==References====External links== As far as the Gas Economic Act is concerned, it was also envisaged to open in several stages in this area. The ElWOG has shown us: the more stages, the greater the inequalities created with them. With regard to one of the key issues in the Gas Economic Act, which is the matter of the Take-or-Pay contracts, I do not wish to continue to carry out this on a time basis, but in this context I would say that the officials of the Ministry of Economic Affairs have been able to find a really successful solution. I can only congratulate you on this, and I believe that this solution is really manageable. I cannot fully understand the position of the gas industry in this context, which regrets the three-party agreement. Perhaps she regrets it because she was not willing to go away from her extreme position, namely a total opening only from 2008. The actual total opening will take place in 2002. I have been told in all cases that their negotiators have agreed to this draft of the Gas Economic Act with the government. To sum up, I believe, it should be mentioned that the present Gas Economic Act will create a compact, clear and clear law, which will bring many opportunities for customers, including consumers, but also for those in the gas industry who are doing more than risk. Finally, I would like to thank the officials for their great support in the negotiations, I thank the government party negotiators, and I would particularly like to thank the negotiator, Mr Head, especially for his fair negotiation. Ladies and gentlemen! We agree with the government template, including the amendments, in the sense of a further, constructive opposition policy! We cannot and will not be in agreement with the government template with which the Federal Constitutional Act, which regulates the ownership of the Austrian electricity industry, is to be repealed. Thank you.

ParlaMint-AT_2000-07-05-021-XXI-NRSITZ-00032_d7e26540	Mr Mitterlehner is next speaker. - Please.
ParlaMint-AT_2000-07-05-021-XXI-NRSITZ-00032_d7e26561	Mr President. Minister! My Dear Ones Ladies and gentlemen! The ElWOG 1 was criticized by the small and medium-sized enterprises, because the opening was only going in the direction of large businesses and was done far too slowly. Now we have a bill that takes into account, above all, the interests of small and medium-sized enterprises as consumers. Considering that the Austrian small and medium-sized enterprises are producing 5 billion kilowatt hours of electricity per year and now pay with the network charges on average about 1.80 s per kilowatt hour, it can be seen that the new regulation – if one also uses the experience of Germany – makes a price reduction in the order of 25 to 30 sizes quite feasible and likely. If you add the chance through shopping cooperatives, then this value could even improve. That is, nationally, we are talking about a price reduction potential in the order of around 2 billion schilling. Therefore, the new ElWOG scheme for the Austrian economy, for small and medium-sized enterprises, is an advantage. It allows for better competition in international comparison. If I liberalise on the one hand – that is clear, and that has already been addressed today – I have to regulate the area of networks by a regulatory authority accordingly. The involvement of small hydropower in the eco-energy system is both new and environmentally-political and, of course, also economic-political. The inclusion of small hydropower in the 8 percent mentioned is therefore very welcome from our point of view. What has not yet been addressed is certification – in this respect Austria has a pioneering role. This was explicitly addressed by the EU Commission in the draft directive on the promotion of renewable energy. In my opinion, certification is an opportunity and at the same time a danger. The danger is that the resolution could become somewhat bureaucratic. The chance is that, in the medium term, a European solution will be possible, with which Austria and other countries will benefit from the respective advantages in the field of energy generation through appropriate certification and thus contribute to meaningful competition.
